I mean, I haven’t been tracking it or anything, and sleep is one of those things where if you ask me directly, I start second-guessing myself — like, was last night typical, or was that just because I had things on my mind?\n\nI don’t think I can point to a clear pattern, like “I always wake up at 4” or “I can’t fall asleep every night.” It’s more that some nights feel better than others, but I haven’t paid enough attention to say it’s a real problem. If you mean whether sleep is the main thing bothering me, I don’t think so. At least, it hasn’t stood out to me the way the concentration and the flat feeling have.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a decrease in the amount of pleasure or interest you get from activities, even ones you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yes… I have. That’s actually one of the things that’s bothered me the most, because it’s not like I stopped doing everything. I still go over to the community center, and I still go out into my garden, but it doesn’t feel the same.\n\nThose used to be the things that lifted me up a little, even on an ordinary day. At the center I’d usually feel better being around people, having little conversations, helping with whatever needed doing. And with the garden, I used to really get something out of it — noticing a new bloom, checking the soil, fussing over plants like they were my little projects. Now I’ll be there doing it, but it feels like I’m just moving through the steps. I smile and talk and do what I’m supposed to do, but inside there’s this dull heaviness most days, like something is sitting on me.\n\nIt’s not that I hate those things now. I don’t. I still care about them, I think. But the pleasure part… it’s muted, or missing. Like I’m present, but not really taking it in the way I used to.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly than usual, or maybe feeling restless and unable to sit still for very long?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know that I’ve noticed that, no. I mean… I wouldn’t say I’m pacing around or that I can’t sit still. And I don’t think anyone’s told me I’m moving especially slowly, either.\n\nThere are times I feel like it takes more effort to get myself going, but that feels more like… I don’t know, like I’m dragging myself through the day emotionally, not necessarily that my body is moving slower. If you were watching me, I’m not sure you’d see anything obvious.\n\nI guess I’d have to think about it more. I haven’t really been paying attention to my movements that way.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite or weight lately — like eating much less or more than usual, or losing or gaining weight without trying?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in any clear way. I mean, my appetite has never been perfectly the same day to day, so it’s hard for me to know what counts as a “change.” Some days I might not feel especially interested in food, and other days I eat normally just because it’s mealtime and that’s what I do.\n\nBut I haven’t noticed my clothes fitting differently or anything like that, and I haven’t been trying to lose weight or worrying that I’ve gained a lot. I don’t really weigh myself regularly, so I can’t give you a number. If there has been a change, it hasn’t jumped out at me. It’s not something I’ve been focused on.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about hurting yourself or that life isn’t worth living lately?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t really know how to answer that neatly. I haven’t sat there making a plan to hurt myself, if that’s what you mean. It’s not like I’ve been thinking, “I’m going to do something.” But sometimes, when I’m really worn down, I do get this thought like… what is the point of all this, you know? Like life feels very heavy and repetitive, and I wonder how long I’m supposed to keep feeling that way.\n\nI don’t think of it as wanting to die exactly. It’s more like wishing I could get a break from being myself for a while, if that makes sense. And then I feel guilty even saying that, because I know I have responsibilities and people would be upset, and I don’t want to scare anyone. But the thoughts do come up in that vague way sometimes — more “I’m tired of this” than “I’m going to hurt myself.”\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way — the heaviness, the lack of interest, and the trouble concentrating?\n\nPatient: It’s been… a little over two years now, I think. It started after my close friend died. I don’t mean that every single day has been exactly the same since then, but that’s when this heaviness really settled in, and it just never fully lifted.\n\nAt first I kept telling myself it was just grief, and maybe it was, at least in the beginning. But then months went by, and then a year, and now it’s been more than two years, and I still feel like I’m carrying it around. The lack of interest, the difficulty focusing… those have kind of been tied up with it since then. I keep thinking I should be more “back to normal” by now, but I’m not.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced periods where you felt unusually high, extremely energized, or unusually irritable — where you were doing or saying things that were out of character for you, maybe not sleeping much but still feeling rested, or getting involved in activities more than usual?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like that. I mean, I’ve had times where I’m more keyed up or irritable, sure, especially when I’m stressed or when there’s too much coming at me, but I wouldn’t say I’ve had periods where I felt unusually “high” or full of energy in a way that felt out of character.\n\nThe not sleeping much but still feeling rested part especially doesn’t sound like me. If I don’t sleep, I usually feel it. And I can’t think of a time where I was suddenly doing a bunch of impulsive things or talking in a way people would say was really unlike me. I’m pretty consistent, maybe too consistent. If anything, I tend to get stuck or worn down rather than revved up.\n\nSo… no, I don’t think I’ve had anything like what you’re describing. At least nothing that stands out to me as a separate “period” or episode.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you’ve completely lost interest in the things you used to enjoy, or do you still care about them even if you don’t get as much pleasure from them anymore?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say it’s completely gone, no… I mean, I still care about those things in the sense that I know they matter to me. Like the community center — I still go, and part of me does want to be there because it used to make me feel connected and useful. And my garden, I still notice if something needs watering or if a plant is doing well. So it’s not like I’ve stopped caring altogether.\n\nBut most days there’s this dull heaviness sitting with me, and even when I’m in those places that used to lift me up, I feel like I’m just going through the motions. I’ll be at the community center smiling and making small talk, or out in the garden doing what needs doing, but it feels… flatter. Like I’m wearing the expression I’m supposed to wear, if that makes sense. A smile pasted on.\n\nSo I guess the interest is still there somewhere, but the pleasure doesn’t come through the way it used to. It’s more like I’m trying to keep up the routine of being the person who enjoys those things.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you’re starting fewer activities on your own, or that it takes much more effort to get yourself to do even simple things that you used to do without thinking?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, that’s definitely been happening. It’s not always that I decide not to do things, exactly. It’s more like getting started feels so much heavier than it used to.\n\nI can wake up after what should be a full night’s sleep and still feel tired, like I never really got the benefit of it. And then something that used to be just part of my day — making a couple of client calls, returning messages, or going outside to water the tomatoes — can feel like I’ve already spent up most of what I had. By the time I’ve done just a few ordinary things, my body feels like I’ve put in a whole day already.\n\nSo I do start fewer things on my own, I think, because I’m almost calculating the effort before I begin. Even simple things don’t feel automatic anymore. I have to talk myself into them more than I used to.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you’ve lost interest or pleasure in nearly all activities, even ones that used to be meaningful or enjoyable for you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say “nearly all,” because that sounds so complete, and I’m not sure it’s that clean-cut. I still know certain things matter to me. I still go to the community center, and I still spend time in the garden, and those used to be places that really lifted me up.\n\nBut most days, there’s this dull heaviness sitting with me. Even when I’m doing those things that should feel meaningful, I feel like I’m just moving through the steps. I’ll smile and talk to people, or I’ll be out there with the plants, and on the outside I probably look like I’m participating, but inside it feels kind of flat. Like I’ve got a smile pasted on and I’m doing what I’m supposed to do, rather than actually feeling the enjoyment from it.\n\nSo I wouldn’t say I’ve lost interest in absolutely everything, but the pleasure part… that’s definitely much harder to reach than it used to be.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that nothing you do brings you pleasure anymore, or that you’ve completely lost interest in everything, even the smallest things that used to matter to you?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’d put it as “nothing,” exactly. That feels too absolute, and I don’t want to make it sound like I’m completely empty or that I don’t care about anything at all. I do still care. I still know the community center matters to me, and I still know my garden matters to me — even little things, like noticing a plant coming back or seeing someone at the center I’ve known for years.\n\nBut most days, there’s this dull heaviness with me, like it’s sitting right there no matter where I go. And even when I’m in those places that used to really lift me up, I feel like I’m mostly going through the motions. I’ll smile, I’ll chat, I’ll do what I usually do, but it feels like the smile is sort of pasted on. Like I’m performing the part of myself that used to enjoy it.\n\nSo no, I wouldn’t say I’ve completely lost interest in everything. But the pleasure… it’s muted. It doesn’t reach me the way it used to.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you’re experiencing a loss of pleasure or interest in nearly all activities, to the point where it feels like nothing really brings you joy anymore?\n\nPatient: I think… yes, in the sense that joy feels very hard to get to now. I don’t know if I’d say absolutely nothing brings me anything, because that sounds so final, and there are still places and people I care about.\n\nBut most days there’s this dull heaviness sitting with me. Even when I’m at the community center, or out in my garden — places that used to lift me up pretty reliably — I feel like I’m just going through the motions. I’ll do the things I normally do, I’ll talk to people, I’ll tend to the plants, and I can even smile, but it feels like the smile is sort of pasted on.\n\nIt’s not that I don’t remember those things mattering. I do. I know they matter. It’s just that the feeling of enjoyment doesn’t really come through the way it used to. It’s like I’m participating from the outside more than actually feeling it.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you’ve experienced a loss of pleasure or interest in nearly all activities, to the point where it feels like nothing really brings you joy anymore?\n\nPatient: I know I’ve said this a few times, but yes… mostly it feels that way. Not in a perfect, absolute sense, like there’s no tiny spark anywhere, because I don’t think that’s honest. I still go to the community center, and I still spend time in my garden, and those are things that used to really lift me up.\n\nBut most days there’s this dull heaviness sitting with me. It follows me into those places, even the ones that should feel comforting or meaningful. I’ll be at the center talking with people, or I’ll be outside with the plants, doing the things I’ve always done, and I’m aware that I’m supposed to be enjoying it. Sometimes I even smile and carry on like normal.\n\nBut inside it feels like I’m just moving through the motions, with this smile sort of pasted on. Like I’m present, but the joy doesn’t quite reach me the way it used to. So yes, I’d say the pleasure has really faded, even in the things that used to matter most to me."
